4/27/25, 12:29 PM - Gurmeet: Goda masala recipe
Ingredients 
1. Coriander seeds-  2 tbls
2. Red chillies- 8 to 10
3. Cloves- 6
4. Cinnamon- 1.5 inch
5. Black cardamom- 1
6. Green cardamom- 3
7. Mace-1
8. Star anise- 1
9. Blackpepper- 8 to 10
10. Stone flower- 1/2 tbs
11. Cumin seeds-  1 tsp
12 Fennel seeds-  1 tsp
13. Sesame seeds- 1/2 tbs
14 Poppy seeds- 1/2 tbs
15. Garlic - 3 to 4 cloves
16. Red onion- 1 evenly and thinly sliced. 
17. Ground ginger powder- 3/4th tsp
18. Red chilli powder- 1 to 1.5 tsp( kashmiri for color)

Method
1. Heat up a pan add ingredients 1- 10. Slow roast for 2 minutes or until slightly fragrant. 
2. Now add cumin seeds+Fennel seeds roast them slightly. All roasting to be done on low to medium heat to prevent burning of the spices.
3. Add sesame +poppy seeds. Now add the garlic cloves toast them until they turn little dry and warm.
4. Set these aside to cool.
5. Either toast the sliced onions in a pan ( 10-12 minutes) until they turn dark brown or dehydrate in an airfryer until crisp. I used the airfryer. 
6. After everything cools down grind the spices( except for the onions and garlic). I used the pulse setting Add the ginger powder+red chilli powder as well.
7. Add the onions and garlic. Pulse in intervals of 30-40 seconds. Store in an airtight container. <This message was edited>

5/4/25, 1:06 PM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: A very simple raw mango chutney. In fact I always use raw mango in my green chutney as well as pani poori pani. 
1/2 cup green mango - peeled and sliced, 1 cup chopped cilantro, I/2 cup fresh mint leaves, 1-2 green chillies, 1/2”piece ginger, juice of 1/2 lemon, roasted jeera powder, black salt to taste. Grind everything together to a smooth paste. You should freeze the remaining chutney in an ice cube tray to preserve the fresh taste.
5/4/25, 1:11 PM - +1 (847) 830-4555: I make the above with a spoonful of flax seeds for added thickness and nutrients.

5/4/25, 2:01 PM - Gurmeet: I can't remember but someone had asked me for the Tofu bhurji recipe. 
Ingredients 
1. Soft tofu( water drained)
2. Cumin seeds
3. Garli chopped- 3
4. Onions chopped
5. Any garam masala of your choice- I used misal masala
6. Red chilli powder+turmeric+coriander cumin powder
7. Green chilli 
 8.tomato
9. Any leafy greens and vegetables of your choice. I used spinach, baby kale leaves, dill leaves, bell pepper, mushroom, peas.
10. Coriander to garnish.
Method-
Dry tempering of cumin seeds and onions+green chilli. Followed by tomatoes. Cook for 5 minutes. Add the masalas followed by vegetables. Cook until the burji dries up a little bit and enjoy. 
A great combination is - burji, Ezekiel crisps, and top it will green chutney. Super delicious and it satisfies your cravings for savory snacks :)) <This message was edited>
5/4/25, 2:03 PM - Gurmeet: <Media omitted>
5/4/25, 7:07 PM - +91 94266 14871: Try this one also. Peel off raw Mango, cut in small pieces, steam or boil till soft. Remember not very soft. Drain water and add salt, chilli powder, turmeric and coriander powder. For tadka use any oil of your choice, mustard is good, heat oil add mustard seeds, cumin , Fenugreek seeds, hing, fennel seeds and add boiled mango and spices . Add jaggery at last for Sweetness. Consistency should be semi liquid. Add black salt at the end. This sweet tange chutney you can enjoy with paratha or anything. Drained water after boiling mango can be used separately for aam panna or simply for panipuri.

Here in California - home ambient temperature in ~ 20 deg C may not ideal for curdling : optimal for bacterial proliferation is ~ 40 - 50 C (~ 100 - 120 F)

Hence one definitely has to heat the milk to lukewarm or slightly above (though much below boiling !

5/19/25, 7:57 AM - +1 (408) 931-0641: Do you still have same gastric issues with green juicing? Since you want to increase nutrition
5/19/25, 8:24 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: I sometimes eat raw greens, and sometimes eat cooked greens. On some days, I have eaten 100% cooked greens. For example, if I am eating hard greens like kale and collards, I feel comfortable steaming them in a microwave and mixing in some steamed tubers, and some interesting herbs & spices. 

Cooking our greens has an advantage: we tend to eat more leafy greens if we cook them. Also, cooking breaks apart cell walls of these leafy greens, thereby releasing nutrition packed inside them.

On the whole, I don't necessarily do 100% raw leafy greens every day -- I feel comfortable consuming them in any combination: raw or cooked.

My main goal: eat as many leafy greens as possible on any given day: cooked or raw. And try to eat a few different leafy greens instead of only one.

5/28/25, 5:51 PM - +61 422 870 898: <Media omitted>
5/28/25, 7:15 PM - +1 (847) 769-6793: Can you share oven cooking instructions
5/28/25, 8:00 PM - Gurmeet: For the dahi wada I used whole urad daal. 
1. Soak the daal overnight or for 6 hours.
2. Add some blackpepper corns, hing, green chilli, and ginger.( I have added chopped onions in the past) Grind this to a thick fluffy batter. Add ice cold water while grinding. You don't want the batter to heat up may result in hard wadas.
3. Ferment this batter overnight. 
4. For the baking method- preheat the oven to 350 F
5. I used a muffin tin lined with muffin cups. ( silicon muffin tin would be ideal) Lightly brush some water on the muffin paper cups.
6. Pour the batter and bake for approximately 15 minutes or until a skewer comes out clean. Let the wadas cool a little bit then gently remove them from the paper cups.  
7. Soak the wadas in warm water for 5-7 minutes. 
8. Squeeze all the water out.
9. Add them to the dahi.

To flavor the dahi
Add some red chilli powder, garam masala powder, sumac, amchoor, cumin powder, and black salt.

6/5/25, 4:03 AM - +1 (416) 998-0434: Do you make almond yogurt at home? Please share the recipe for making the yogurt.
6/5/25, 4:31 AM - Uma Venkataraman WFPB: @⁨~Manisha⁩, I have made almond yogurt but found it only worked when I added some thickener. 

Soak 1.5 cups almonds for about 2 hours or till plumped up. Add 4 cups cold water or ice/cold water combo and blend in a high speed blender like a Vitamix for 2 minutes. Strain and use the pulp for other recipes. This just uses the milk. Put the cold milk into a saucepan. 

Dissolve thickener (1/2 Tbsp arrowroot powder & 1/2 Tbsp agar agar) into some cold almond milk and incorporate completely. Mix into the entire almond milk and heat on a medium heat till it is almost boiling but don’t let it boil over. 

Let cool to baby bath water temperature to put in a yogurt starter and set. 

I leave in the instant pot for 16 to 24 hours. 24 gets it very tart but it I really great for khadi etc. 

I played around a lot with thickeners but this was my favorite. 1/2 Tbsp corn starch & 1/2 tsp xanthan gum was also a good thickener but I liked the agar agar-arrowroot powder the best.

6/6/25, 12:01 PM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: I cook and freeze the food then put it in hard coolers just before leaving. I have frozen water in small bottles which I’ll keep in the coolers to keep things cool. It lasts for the first 2-4 days. Then I’ll make simple quick things like khichri, salads etc. The burritos (especially if no cheese) last 3-4 days easily in the coolers.  

All hotels we stay in overnight have fridges so I transfer things to them at night. If they have larger fridges, I keep the food in the freezers. I’ll fill the bottles or containers with ice from the hotels every morning for the ongoing journey.

We like variety in our meals 😀😋 <This message was edited>

Optional- garnish with almond slivers or pista.

6/8/25, 8:13 PM - Leena Menon WFPB: Deviled Potatoes

Potatoes
Satvic cashew cheese 
Roasted bell pepper 
For the brine:
1 tbsp black pepper
0.25c soy sauce
1 cup rice vinegar
1 cup water

Brine potatoes overnite. 
Give the potatoes a boil in the brine water then drain. 
Slice potatoes
Layer in pan and bake at 370 for 25 mins. Turn off the oven. 
Top with cheese and roasted bell pepper. 
Put back in the oven for 10 mins. 

Satvic cheese  
1 cup cashews soaked in hot water for 30 mins 
1.5 cup boiled cauliflower 
3/4 tsp salt 
4 tbsp nutritional yeast
1 tbsp ACV
Grind with 1.5 cup water <This message was edited>

6/18/25, 8:52 AM - Prathima WFPB: Potato dapati or hashbrowns :

Base recipe:
Raw grated potato(use small grater), approx 2-3
Chopped Coriander leaves, 3-4tsp
Chopped Green chili, 1-2
Grated coconut(this is part of original recipe, I skipped it), 2 tsp
Roasted groundnut crushed/powder, 3-4tbsp
Salt, as per taste
Cumin seeds, 1tsp
Chili powder, optional

Mix everything together and tap on  iron tawa. Cook on low flame with a closed lid on both sides.

Options: any other grated veggies and greens can be added but potatoes should be higher in quantity.

6/19/25, 9:36 AM - Gurmeet: Will do. It was actually just an attempt to use the residue from juicing carrots 🥕 yesterday. However,  you can make carrot kheer in a similar way. It has 1. makhana( soaked in warm milk for 20-30 minutes), 
2. Grated carrots 
3. Soy milk for soaking makhanna and cooking the kheer in
4. Cardamom powder
5. Hemp hearts 
6. Date sugar
7. Dates- to add to the kheer and to garnish
8. Cashews- to garnish
9. Soaked and partially cooked chana daal( I did not add this but realized later that this addition would make the kheer taste even better)
I cooked all of the above for 15- 20 minutes stirring occasionally until the kheer thickened. Then added the makhana and cooked for another 10 minutes. That's it:)

6/19/25, 6:19 PM - Divya CMU: Looks yum. How do you make tamarind chutney
6/19/25, 6:37 PM - Divya CMU: A few people asked for the recipe. Here you go 

1. Steam veggies you like on one side. We steamed like carrots corn bean spinach zucchini and brussel sprouts  garlic cloves. 
2. Wash and keep mushroom capsicum aside or any other veggie that you don't want to steam but add to curry
3. In a pan add finely chopped tomato garlic ginger and saute, cool it down and grind it into a paste.
4. Add puree to the pan, add non steamed veggies, add coconut milk, saute for a while then add masala like cumin turmeric lemon grass pepper salt  steamed veggies 
5. Cook well and enjoy with brown rice or quinoa
6/19/25, 7:26 PM - +1 (972) 876-8806: Thank you
6/19/25, 7:44 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Recipe is awesome! ✨ Delighted to see a Thai recipe being developed ✨

A note about coconuts: *in our WhatsApp group, we discourage coconuts*: for technical details, we may browse through this article (which has slides & links to several other articles): https://thankful2plants.com/nuts-seeds/coconuts/index.html

*Coconut Substitutes*: At the bottom of the above article, there is a section titled “Coconut Substitutes” in which members of our recipe sharing group have shared ideas.

*WFPB Chit Chat group*: After reading the above article, some individuals may be keen to discuss saturated fats, MCTs,  EFAs, Low Fat guideline, … Such discussions become technical in nature and become a distraction for a recipe sharing group. For such discussions, we have a separate group called “WFPB Chit Chat”! We may join that group by clicking on this link: https://chat.whatsapp.com/G3NbxtiAazL7L1IP38CVFe 🙏
6/19/25, 7:46 PM - Prathima WFPB: I used 1 cup whole wheat flour, 1 cup almond flour, 1/2tsp baking powder, salt, 1 cup water. Kneading it into a dough. Rested it for 15-30mins.
Made cashew butter using 1 cup cashews.

I rolled the dough by spreading cashew butter on it and folded and repeated the process for 5-6 times. Kept the rolled one in freezer for 20mins. after that rolled it again couple of times and folded by spreading cashew butter.
Finally rolled it into a thick chapathi dough consistency.
Stuffing :
Nuts and seeds chopped, black raisins, dried pineapple pieces, dates piece, dates paste, cardamom powder, kesar etc.

Put the stuffing between 2 round rolled dough like in photo.

Baked it in airfrier for 30mins below 180°c by turning at half the time.

The dough can be used to make pies, puffs etc too.
6/19/25, 7:47 PM - Prathima WFPB: <Media omitted>
6/19/25, 7:48 PM - Prathima WFPB: This was a savory version with the above dough. Stuffing was made by roasting onions, tofu, bell pepper, basil, salt and chili powder, coriander leaves. My daughter loved this one.
6/19/25, 7:54 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: *Coconut Substitutes*: Coconuts are a cherished food for many of us! But coconuts are discouraged by WFPB luminaries like Ornish, Barnard, Esselstyn, McDougall, Peter Rogers, …! What to do? Here are some ideas by members of our recipe sharing group:

* _"I am a Mangalorean so traditionally we use a lot of coconut in our cooking...1 way of reducing the quantity of coconut consumption in the cooking is by grinding Silken tofu with maybe 1 tbsp or 2 of coconut blend it and u will get the desired coconut flavor and consistency that u need for ur south indian gravy dishes"_ — Sharmila Vedam  (@⁨~Sharmila⁩) [PCRM Food For Life Coach]

* _"I grind a tiny amount of coconut (for flavor) with roasted daliya [taste and texture is same as coconut based gravy.         But no matter which essence I buy, the only flavor I get is that of KoprKopra matter which essence I buy, the only flavor I get is that of Koprayonut)."_ — Padma (@⁨Padma WFPB⁩)

* _"A surprising substitute I stumbled upon recently. Fig leaves have a hint of coconut in their flavor. More young coconut flavor than mature coconut. I used it to wrap food I was steaming as I’d run out but I have a small fig tree in the garden. I used it in a dessert."_ — Uma (@⁨Uma Venkataraman WFPB⁩)

* _"Celery (plus hemp or cashews) is a great substitute for coconut. I use itAmbikahe time in koottu, chutneys, and pachadis (various sauce like dishes in s.Indian cuisine). WilAmbikaout sambar and share if it turns out good. For sambar, coconut is not necessary if fresh masala is made, so have not tried celery there as yet."_ — Ambika (@⁨~Ambika⁩)

* _"For south indian ‘morekuzhambu’ soaked raw peanuts also work.     _ — AmbikaowlAmbikaceeeds while blending which usually masks the flavor of coconut substitutes i use. I would slowlAmbikace the coconut and substitute with alternate stuff until you strike the right taste for you."_ — Ambika (@⁨~Ambika⁩)

6/22/25, 7:56 AM - Gurmeet: <Media omitted>
6/22/25, 8:04 AM - Gurmeet: Someone had asked me for the tamarind date chutney. Heres the recipe- 
Tamarind - 1/2 cup soaked in 1 cup water and pulp seperated.
Dates- 5-6 Medjool dates
Chilli powder- 1 tsp or to taste.
Cumin powder- 2 tsp
Coriander powder- 1.5 tsp
Garam masala powder- 2 tsp or adjust as per taste. 
Date sugar- 2 tsp or adjust as per taste:)
1. Add some water to the  tamarind pulp and bring to boil.
2. Lower the heat and let this cook for 5-7 minutes until the mixture thickens.
3. Add the dates , after cooking for another 6 minutes. Break down the dates with a spoon so it integrates into the chutney. ( I do not strain out the dates. I prefer a thick chutney with some date pieces in it)
4. Add the masalas.  Adjust the masalas and the thickness as per personal preference. 
5. Enjoy :)) <This message was edited>
6/22/25, 9:20 AM - Gurmeet: <Media omitted>
6/22/25, 9:20 AM - Gurmeet: <Media omitted>
6/22/25, 1:58 PM - Gurmeet: <Media omitted>
6/22/25, 2:02 PM - Gurmeet: <Media omitted>
6/22/25, 2:09 PM - +1 (972) 672-3725: Recipe pls?
6/22/25, 2:30 PM - Kusum WFPB: Looks delicious sour and tangy
6/22/25, 2:43 PM - Gurmeet: Ingredients 
1. Tamarind- 1/4 cup tamarind soaked in 1 cup water and squeezed the pulp. Use the tamarind  liquid.  
2. Black chana leftover water- 1/2 cup
3. Beetroot leftover water- 1/4 cup
4. Mustard seeds- 1/2 tsp
5. Cumin seeds- 1/2 tsp
6. Asafoetida- a pinch
7. Curry leaves- 2sprigs
8. Green chilli- 3
9. Tomato- 1  cubed
10. Rasam powder- 1 tbls or to taste. 
11. Cilantro- for garnish.
12.  Frozen cranberries - 5-6
13. Garlic- 1 chopped
Method-
1. Dry temper the mustard +cumin seeds. 
2. Add green chillies, Asafoetida and curry leaves. 
3. Add the tomatoes, cranberries, and the tamarind water. Bring to a boil and cook on simmer for 5 minutes. The tomatoes should soften a little bit. 
4. Add the black chana and beetroot water along with rasam powder, cilantro, and garlic.  Cook for 5-7 minutes. 
5. Enjoy!!
** I added the black chana water and beetroot water as I had it. We can skip if we don't have it.

6/28/25, 6:20 PM - +1 (416) 998-0434: My mom made this one.
Gatta: besan, salt, ajwain, red chilli powder, a pinch of salt, add half soy yogurt & half water to make the dough. Dough should be a bit firmer than roti dough. Roll the dough into finger thick sticks>boil until cooked>cut into desired shape
Curry: tadka of mustard seeds, curry patta, green chillies, add mix of sour yogurt & boiled gatta water (from above)> keep stirring until boils, simmer until yougurt is cooked>add gatta> simmer for a few minutes. Garnish with coriander leaves. 
Variation of Curry - 1)onion, garlic & tomato paste 2)tomato paste with or without yogurt as above 3) regular yogurt kadhi

6/29/25, 12:55 PM - Gurmeet: Pav bhaji masala powder-
Ingredients 
• 4 tbsp coriander seeds / dhaniya seeds
• 2 tbsp cumin seeds / jeera
• 2 bay leaf / tej patta
• 10 cloves / lavang
• 2 cardamom / elachi
• 2 inch cinnamon stick / dalchini
• 1 tbsp pepper / kali mirch
• ¾ tbsp saunf / fennel seeds
• 10 dried kashmiri red chili / lal mirch
• 1 tsp turmeric powder / haldi
• 1 tbsp dry mango powder / aamchur powder
• 1.5 star anise
• 1/2 tsp dry ginger powder 
• 1.5 black cardamom/junglee velchi 
• 1 tbls Red chilli powder
• 1tsp Turmeric

Dry roast the ingredients- 
1. start with star anise, roast until a floral aroma arises. Remove this from the pan
2. Now add the coriander and cumin seeds. Roast this and remove from the pan.
3. Add the rest of the spices exclude the powders. Roast set aside to cool.
4. Blend this into a powder

6/30/25, 11:22 AM - Gurmeet: This is a quick smoothie. Tried it with apples for the first time, typically I use banana.  Here's the recipe, in case anyone would like to give it a try. 
1. I soaked 2 tbs hemp hearts in the grinder jar in 1/4th cup of water for 5-7 minutes.  
2. Blended this to make the milk.
3. Added 2 Medjool dates, 1 elachi,  1/2 an apple to the jar. Blended again.
4. Make a slurry of the millet powder- 2 tbs in  3/4th cup water. Then cook this until it thickens. 
5. Let this cool, add to the blender jar to make smoothie. 
6. You may top it with soaked basil seeds. 
This is good for 2 people.

7/7/25, 6:19 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: * 8-10 raw dates [my brother bought some raw dates; we didn’t know what to do with them; we made a chutney)
* ⁠7-8 regular dates (deseeded, soaked in water)
* ⁠1 Tbsp golden berries (tart, soaked, if not available then add 1 Tbsp raw mango)
* ⁠1 tsp black pepper
* ⁠1/2 tsp cinnamon
* ⁠1/2 tsp roasted cumin powder
* ⁠1 tsp vinegar
* ⁠1 tsp lemon
* ⁠1 tsp amchoor powder (raw mango powder)
Adjust quantities of black pepper, tart ingredients (golden berries, raw mango, raw mango powder, lemon, …) according to taste.

7/8/25, 8:16 AM - Satvic Swetha Kogatam: Woww ! Looks very yumm ! I was thinking about making kheer today . Can you pls share the process too pls
7/8/25, 8:17 AM - Gurmeet: Sure! Give me 15 minutes:)) This will be kids breakfast as well. Hoping they like it :)
7/8/25, 9:16 AM - Gurmeet: Amaranth- 11/2 cups( soaked overnight)
Date paste- 4 tbls
Cardamom- 2 powdered
Soy milk- 11/2 cup
Water- 2 cups
Mango pulp- 1/2 cup
Almond butter- 1 tbls
Makhanna- 10-12
Figs- 2
Dry fruits- to garnish
Fresh fruits- any of your choice ( I added mango, peaches, plums)
Dry fruits- raisins, dried cherries
Method
Put all the ingredients except for the mango pulp and garnishing into the IP. Pressure cook for 20 minutes. 
After the pressure is released add the mango pulp, stir it in, adjust the sweetness. 
Dish it out in bowls and garnish with your toppings and enjoy!! <This message was edited>

7/8/25, 9:13 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Yeah, we may use organic noodles, as long as they are made from whole grains (and not polished grains). 
* For example, buckwheat noodles (also known as soba noodles), whole wheat noodles, or brown rice noodles, and so on. These are made from whole grains like buckwheat, whole wheat and brown rice.
* ⁠We avoid glass noodles (made from starch extracts, which are not whole foods), rice noodles (typically made from polished rice — not a whole grain), and so on.

When buying noodles, it is helpful to study the ingredient list and assess: are they made from whole grains (whole wheat or brown rice or buckwheat or …) , or polished grains (white rice), or starch extracts (potato starch, for example)? <This message was edited>

7/10/25, 8:57 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Baingan Pakoda

1 small brinjal — slice into small pieces.

BATTER:
2 Tbsp besan
1/2 tsp black pepper
1 tsp amchoor
1 tsp Pancha Phoron spice mix (ground)
1 tsp garlic, crushed
1 tsp dried kasoori methi
A pinch of ajwain (carom seeds) - rubbed between fingers
2 tsp cilantro (coriander leaves), chopped
Optionally add minced green chili or chili flakes.

1. Mix batter ingredients together. Add water 1 tsp at a time. Keep mixing into a batter that is neither too hard nor too runny.
2. Coat brinjal siices with the batter on all sides. Air fry for 10 mins each side at 180 C.
3. Serve with green chutney, date chutney, lemon & our favorite spice mixes (Chaat Masala, for example).

7/11/25, 8:01 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Oatmeal, apple and carrots! I don't eat any sugar at all! No flour
Ingredients:
2 cups (200 g) rolled oats, chopped in a blender
1 teaspoon baking powder
1 cup (240 ml) plant based milk of your choice
1 carrot, grated
9 dried apricots, rinsed with hot water and chopped
4 tablespoons raisins, rinsed with hot water
1 apple, grated

I found this, hope it helps:
Here's a step-by-step guide to help you bake this healthy, sugar-free, flourless oatmeal, apple, and carrot bake.

Optional (but helpful additions):

1 tsp cinnamon (great with apple & carrot)
½ tsp vanilla extract (optional)
Pinch of salt (to balance flavors)
1–2 tbsp chia seeds or flaxseed meal (optional, helps bind and adds fiber)

👩‍🍳 Instructions:

1. Preheat Oven. Set oven to 180°C (350°F)

2. Prepare Oats: Blend rolled oats briefly to break them down — like coarse flour or fine flakes.  Combine with baking powder (and salt/spices if using) in a mixing bowl.

3. Mix Wet Ingredients: In another bowl, mix grated carrot, grated apple, milk, chopped apricots, and raisins. Let sit for 5–10 minutes — oats will absorb moisture better.

4. Combine Everything: Mix the wet and dry ingredients together. The batter should be thick but spoonable. If it's too dry, add a little more milk.

5. Pour into Baking Dish: Use a small baking dish or loaf pan (lined with parchment).  Flatten the top with a spatula.

6. Bake for 35–40 minutes, or until set and lightly golden on top. It’s done when a toothpick in the center comes out mostly clean.

7. Cool at least 15–20 minutes before slicing — it firms up as it cools.

🧊 Storage: Store in the fridge up to 5 days. Freezes well too.

Serving Suggestions: Enjoy as a breakfast bar, snack, or healthy dessert. <This message was edited>

7/15/25, 7:13 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Two interesting quotes from this video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qWBK9FQ0jxY
* _”*Salt has always been considered The Lazy Man's way of flavoring meals because it overpowers everything!* You can't taste the other subtleties in your foods and in your seasonings when salt is involved. When you reduce that or eliminate it, you will taste food in a way that you have never tasted food before. So do not leave out the herbs and spices. Use these liberally! Not only are they flavorful but they're so beneficial!"_
* ⁠_”There is a lot of options out there. You just have to get creative and step outside the box. We're so used to using salt and pepper and hot sauce and different ways to cover up the flavor of the meat and the dairy. But you don't have to do that anymore! Now you can enjoy the wonderful subtleties and boldness of plant-based foods."_
7/15/25, 7:15 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Six ideas are shared in this video above (to reduce or eliminate salt in our meals):
1. Naturally high acidic foods like lemon; we may use vinegars too.
2. ⁠Salt-free mustard.
3. ⁠Vegetables with naturally higher sodium, e.g., celery and broccoli.
4. ⁠Naturally spicy foods like cayenne pepper, chipotle pepper, berbere spice mix, red pepper flakes, jalapenos, chile peppers; ginger is also spicy and spikes up flavor.
5. ⁠Herbs & dried spices like onion powder, garlic powder and black pepper. Minced garlic, fresh onion. Herbs like tyme, oregano, basil, paprika, cumin, rosemary, mint — fresh or dried.
6. ⁠Sea vegetables (nori, dulse) and veggie stock.
7/15/25, 7:26 PM - +1 (510) 449-2657: Is Veggie stock whole food? I think it is extracts of vegetables.
7/15/25, 7:36 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: In Whole Food Plant-Based circles, some non whole foods are considered acceptable:
1. Cacao powder — these are defattened cacao beans, not whole cacao beans.
2. ⁠Tofu & Tempeh — these are not whole foods.
3. ⁠Plant-Based milks — almost all plant based milks are non whole foods; they involve a filtration process; at home, we may use a nut bag for filtration.
4. ⁠Teas — we typically throw away green tea leaves even though we can chew them; matcha powder is ground green tea leaves.
5. ⁠⁠Veggie broth — these may be considered “veggie extracts” (non whole foods): we boil veggies in water, retain the liquid and discard the solids. Similar to making tea.
6. Vegetable juices — they are considered health promoting; at the same time, Dr Greger reminds us that whole foods (veggie smoothies) instead of vegetable juices are nutritionally superior. <This message was edited>

7/16/25, 12:44 PM - Gurmeet: I cooked ragi in water, added  soy milk to it, some almond powder, date syrup, date sugar, cardamom powder. Cook this for about 30 minutes, stirring continuously.  The mixture will thicken. Pour this in a cake baking dish, garnish with cashews, cover and refrigerate for 2-4 hours. Invert it in a plate and cut out squares. Made this for Mom, satva was one of her favorite desserts growing up. The original recipe is made in coconut milk. Thankfully she liked this version of it. :))

7/16/25, 1:30 PM - Gurmeet: I was eyeballing everything. I will try to recollect and share it :)
7/16/25, 2:17 PM - Gurmeet: Here's the recipe
Goan Satv
Ingredients 
1. Ragi- 5 tbls
2. Water- 1.5 cups
3. Soy milk- 1 cup
4. Almond flour- 1.5 tbls
5. Cardamom- 3 powdered
6. Date syrup- 3 tbls
7. Date sugat- 3 tbls
8. Cashews- to garnish

Method 
1. Add the ragi and water to a pot mix the ragi with the water. 
2. Place the pot on the stove and allow this to come to a gentle boil on slow to medium heat while whisking the mix.
3. Add the rest of the ingredients and continue to stir with the whisk until the mix thickens and turns shiny. At this point check to see if the sweetness is to your liking. It took me between 25-30 minutes of continuous stirring to attain the desired thickness. The mix should be as thick as idli batter. 
4. Pour this in a 2 to 3 inch depth container. I used a bread baking tray.
5. Place the tray in the refrigerator for 2 to 3 hours. 
6. Remove from refrigerator, run a knife through the edges to loosen the satv.
7. Invert this into a plate, cut into squares, garnish with cashew bits and enjoy.
****keep the rest refrigerated. <This message was edited>

7/16/25, 8:16 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: *Coconut Substitutes*: Coconuts are a cherished food for many of us! But coconuts are discouraged by WFPB luminaries like Ornish, Barnard, Esselstyn, McDougall, Peter Rogers, …! What to do? Here are some ideas by members of our recipe sharing group:

* _"I am a Mangalorean so traditionally we use a lot of coconut in our cooking...1 way of reducing the quantity of coconut consumption in the cooking is by grinding Silken tofu with maybe 1 tbsp or 2 of coconut blend it and u will get the desired coconut flavor and consistency that u need for ur south indian gravy dishes"_ — Sharmila Vedam  (@⁨~Sharmila⁩) [PCRM Food For Life Coach]

* _"I grind a tiny amount of coconut (for flavor) with roasted daliya [taste and texture is same as coconut based gravy.         But no matter which essence I buy, the only flavor I get is that of KoprKopra matter which essence I buy, the only flavor I get is that of Koprayonut)."_ — Padma (@⁨Padma WFPB⁩)

* _"A surprising substitute I stumbled upon recently. Fig leaves have a hint of coconut in their flavor. More young coconut flavor than mature coconut. I used it to wrap food I was steaming as I’d run out but I have a small fig tree in the garden. I used it in a dessert."_ — Uma (@⁨Uma Venkataraman WFPB⁩)

* _"Celery (plus hemp or cashews) is a great substitute for coconut. I use itAmbikahe time in koottu, chutneys, and pachadis (various sauce like dishes in s.Indian cuisine). WilAmbikaout sambar and share if it turns out good. For sambar, coconut is not necessary if fresh masala is made, so have not tried celery there as yet."_ — Ambika (@⁨~Ambika⁩)

* _"For south indian ‘morekuzhambu’ soaked raw peanuts also work.     _ — AmbikaowlAmbikaceeeds while blending which usually masks the flavor of coconut substitutes i use. I would slowlAmbikace the coconut and substitute with alternate stuff until you strike the right taste for you."_ — Ambika (@⁨~Ambika⁩)

Two posts in this Whole Food Plant-Based support group on Facebook discuss coconut substitutes:

7/17/25, 12:12 PM - Shalini WFPB: What was your receipie for popped amaranth laddo Kiran ?
7/17/25, 12:34 PM - Gurmeet: Amaranth ladoos 
Ingredients 
1. Amaranth- 2 cups popped ( some extra to roll the ladoos in)
2. Dates- 10 to 12 soaked in about 4 tbls warm water.
3. Peanuts- 4 tbls roasted 
4. Dried sweet cherries- 2 tbls
5. Soy milk- 1/2 cup
6. Almond flour- 2 tsp
Making the ladoos
1. In a blender add the soaked dates with the water in which they were soaked, cherries, peanuts, and half of the soymilk. Its best to add the milk in batches.  Blend this to a thick paste. Adjust the milk accordingly. 
2. Spoon this mix to a mixing bowl.  Add the amaranth, and almond flour,  mix it well. It should be a cohesive chapati consistency dough.
3. Wet your palms with a little bit of water and roll the ladoos, then roll them in the extra popped amaranth. 
4. Ladoos are ready! Enjoy!

WFPB Notes: (1) Instead of the “Oatly” brand for oat milk, we may choose “Three Trees” brand (found in Grocery Outlet for cheap). Oatly has oil as ingredient; Three Trees has much better ingredient list), (2) Instead of agave syrup or honey or jaggery (all of which get classified as “Added Sugars” in nutrition), we may choose homemade date syrup or date paste as sweetener (dates, date syrup, date sugar & date paste are recommended by Dr Greger; we can also use raisins, dried figs etc. for sweetening our preparations (other than chai)).

7/19/25, 9:51 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Dr Michael Greger is a Whole Food Plant-Based luminary. 

* Dr Greger’s website is NutritionFacts.org where he has posted over 2,000 short videos on nutrition. I have watched approximately 80% to 85% of his videos — they helped me learn a lot about the scientific reasoning underlying these nutrition guidelines. He also does hour long webinars frequently. Recently, his webinars have qualified for CME credits (Continuing Medical Education credits).

* We may check out Dr Greger’s bestseller “How Not To Die”: https://www.amazon.com/How-Not-Die-Discover-Scientifically/dp/1250066115/ (with a whopping 28,000 Amazon ratings).

* ⁠He has some recipe books too: The most popular is “How Not To Die Cookbook”: https://www.amazon.com/How-Not-Die-Cookbook-Recipes/dp/1250127769/ — this recipe book is SOS-Free, without Salt, Oil and Sugar. For a salty flavor, he uses miso in some recipes.

* Dr Greger is Fellow, ACLM and also a founding member of ACLM (American College of Lifestyle Medicine), which has started a new specialization in Western medicine called “Lifestyle Medicine”. Just like we have cardiology, rheumatology, etc as medical specializations, “Lifestyle Medicine” is a similar specialization, requiring board certification. Kaiser Permanente publishes a list of Lifestyle Medicine doctors at their website. 

In Lifestyle Medicine, the emphasis is on lifestyle, especially nutrition! Whole Food Plant-Based nutrition without any animal products (100% vegan) without any Oil, without any Sugar, and with Whole plant foods (no Ultra-Processed Foods, and no refined grains; we consume whole grains).

* If somebody is new to Lifestyle Medicine, it would be best to join the “WFPB Chit Chat” group and seek pointers to literature (books, articles), support groups (FaceBook), and online videos: We may join that group by clicking on this link: https://chat.whatsapp.com/G3NbxtiAazL7L1IP38CVFe This way, our group (“WFPB Recipe Sharing”) can focus on recipe sharing 🙏

* Why did Dr Greger start NutritionFacts website and what motivates him to publish all these videos and write all these books? He shares his personal story in this 3-minute video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=G9Z-gKAvzOY I find his approach and his style of working inspiring. <This message was edited>

7/21/25, 8:44 AM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: *Mango Cheesecake*

This is what I did -

*For the cheese layer* 
1 cup soaked cashews
1 block silken tofu
1/2 block firm tofu
1/2 cup soy yogurt
1/2 cup date syrup
1/4 cup maple syrup
1 tsp pure vanilla extract
1 tsp agar agar (soaked in room temp water for 10 minutes, then boiled in a pan until it begins to form a gel) - psyllium husk could possibly also work
Rind and juice of one large lemon

Blend everything together until smooth. Then pour on top of the base layer. Freeze until set - 3-4 hours or overnight. 

*The base layer* -
1/2 cup walnuts 
1/2 cup pecans (replace with walnuts if not available)
1/4 cup almonds
10 mejdool dates
1/2 cup roasted almond flour

Blend in a food processor till it resembles breadcrumbs. Do not add water. Pour out on the springform pan and spread evenly in a thin layer. Press down firmly with a small bowl or anything that’s flat. Freeze for a couple of hours. 

*Mango layer*-
I used 1/2 frozen kesar mango pulp from India and 1/2 fresh mangoes - about 1 cup each. 

Blend together until smooth and pour on top of the “cheese” layer. Put it back in the freezer for another couple of hours. 

Decorate with cut mango cubes and slivered almonds.

Bring the cheesecake into the fridge an hour before serving. <This message was edited>

7/22/25, 12:23 PM - +1 (416) 998-0434: Kanji: this is how I made it. 
I have made with 1) raw mix diced or shredded veggies (carrot, beet, cabbage, turnip etc.) and 2) boiled diced potatoes. 
Boil water, when it’s warm add salt, turmeric, hing, black mustard powder, red chili powder & acv. Leave it on counter top. You can add the veggies same day or next day. Optional to add grated ginger. 
Cover the jar with cloth & keep in sun for a few hours (if possible). It will start to turn sour on day 3-4. Keep checking & start using. Once it is sour enough for me I keep it outside for a day or 2 & move leftover to fridge. <This message was edited>

7/23/25, 9:43 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Amaranth pudding (with sweet fruits and cardamom powder) is one of my favorite preparations. I have taken it for non-WFPB potlucks in the past. For such potlucks, I would add sweeteners like chopped dates, chopped dried figs, frozen sweet cherries, a little bit of cacao powder, slivered almonds (and other nuts like pistachios) and I would make the amaranth thick.. by continuously stirring it (to avoid amaranth sticking to the bottom of the pot). Every time, ppl were amazed, and they would remark, “This tastes great! And I can’t believe that this has zero sugar!”

7/24/25, 7:43 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: I hope some ppl try this strategy of eating buckwheat (a whole grain): we soak buckwheat overnight. Next day, rinse it well 3-4 times in water. Then add fresh fruit, rehydrated berries, and misc powders like cardamom powder, cinnamon powder, fennel powder, ginger powder (whatever our taste buds like). We may add some dates or dried figs or date syrup or date sugar for extra sweetness.

We don’t cook the buckwheat - it’s eaten raw after overnight soaking (and rinsing 3-4 times). <This message was edited>

7/26/25, 12:55 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Kanji recipes tend to have a lot of salt. For example, this recipe has 3 Tbsp salt in 2.5 liters of kanji. We may browse through this article to study how much salt we should eat: https://thankful2plants.com/salt-sodium/sodium-guidelines/index.html 

A rule of thumb is that we should limit our total salt intake to 1/2 tsp daily or less.  Since 1 Tbsp = 3 tsp, the salt used in 2.5 liters of kanji prepared in the recipe above = total salt needed by 1 person for 6 days = total salt needed by 6 people in 1 day. <This message was edited>

7/26/25, 6:44 PM - +1 (832) 755-3722: Amaranth Thalipeeth

1 cup Rajgira (Amaranth) flour
1 large steamed Sweet potato (Japanese or regular)  
1 carrot grated (I hand grate so it’s easy to blend) 
A handful of arugula leaves chopped fine. 
A handful of cilantro leaves chopped fine Chilies chopped fine according to taste
Peanut or pecan powder 
Salt to taste 

Mash the sweet potato well, add all the ingredients and mix well into a ball. If it’s too mushy, add some more amaranth flour. 
Split into 4-6 portions. 
Heat the tava, I use cast iron that I season with oil and wipe the oil away. With wet hands, either pat each portion one by one directly on the tava, or pat it into a thalipeeth on a silicon mat and gently lift to place on the tava. Cover and let it cook for 2-3 minutes. Flip it and let the other side cook. 
Enjoy the warm with chutney.
7/26/25, 6:46 PM - +1 (832) 755-3722: Green Apple Chutney

1 large Granny Smith apple 
A large handful cilantro 
Chillies to taste 
1 clove garlic
A handful of roasted peanuts or pecans 
Salt 

Grind in a blender until smooth.

7/29/25, 4:39 AM - +267 71 323 117: Looks yum.i have a lot of palm .pls share how you did it
7/29/25, 4:51 AM - Gurmeet: Ingredients 
1. Tri colored quinoa- 1/2 cup
2. Chana daal- 4 tbls
3. Toor daal- 3 tbls
4. Moong daal- 3 tbls
5. Spinach- 1/2 cup
6. Green chillies- 2
7. Ginger- 1/2 inch
8. Garam masala powder- 1 tsp( to taste)
9. Coriander+cumin powder- 1 tsp( to taste)
10. Turmeric powder- 1/3 tsp
11. Himalayan salt- to taste
12. Cumin seeds- 1 tsp
13. Carrots- 1 chopped
14. Green peas- 1/4 cup
15, green beans- 1/4 cup chopped 
16 garlic cloves- 2 chopped

Method
1. Rinse and soak ingredients 1 to 4 for 30 minutes.
2. Blanch the Spinach. Add green chillies, ginger and grind to a paste.
3. Pressure cook the soaked quinoa+daals+turmeric powder, salt until soft. I cooked it for 5 minutes after the first whistle. 
4. In a pot dry temper the cumin, add garlic. Sauté for a minute. 
5. Add the peas and carrots, cook for 5 minutes. 
6. Add the pureed Spinach,  stir it, cook it for a minute or two, add the garam masala, coriander and cumin powder.  Now add the quinoa+daals and stir. 
7. Turn the heat off and enjoy!

7/30/25, 12:40 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: What is the recipe for Banana Pops?
7/30/25, 4:09 AM - Gurmeet: Banana pops ( 30)
Ingredients 
1. Medium bananas - 4
2. Toothpicks- 30
Chocolate sauce
1. Soaked dates- 12
2. Cacao powder- 2 tbls
3. Water- 2/3rd cup
4. Date syrup- 6 tbls
5. Almond butter- 2 tbls
Toppings
1. Popped Amaranth 
Method-
1. Peel the bananas, cut into thick slices, insert toothpick through the center of the slices and freeze for 2-3 hours. 
2. For the chocolate sauce soak the dates in 2/3rd cup hot water for 30 minutes.  
3. Blend the above to make a smooth paste. Add addition water if needed.
4. To a pot add the date syrup to 1/4th cup water and cook on low heat until the mixture thickens a little bit.
5. To this add the blended chocolate date mix and the almond butter. Stir and let this cook for 1-2 minutes.
6. Turn off the heat, let the chocolate sauce cool down then pour into a glass bowl.
7. For the banana pops, dip each banana slice into the sauce, then gently roll into the popped amaranth bowl. Or sprinkle copped  nuts. ( described in additional toppings)
8. Drizzle the extra sauce on the pops, freeze them for 20 minutes and serve.

Additionally topic suggestion-
1. Pistachios- 2 tbls roasted and crushed.
2. Peanuts - 2 tbls roasted and crushed.
3. Sesame seeds- 1 tbls roasted <This message was edited>

8/1/25, 2:01 PM - Gurmeet: Oil Free Overnight Focaccia

Ingredients 
1. Active dry yeast- 1 tsp
2. Maple syrup- 1 tsp
3. Whole wheat flour- 2 1/4 th cups +2 tbls
( I used One degree organic sprouted Whole Wheat Flour)
Method- 
1. Measure 11/4th cups warm ( 105F) water into a measuring cup, stir in yeast and maple syrup. Cover the bowl and place it in a dark place for 1-2 hrs. The yeast will foam up and the liquid will get cloudy. 
2. In a medium bowl stir together 2 1/4th cups of the flour. Stir yeast mixture into flour mixture  until you have a batter- like dough. Stir dough vigorously with a spatula or wooden spoon for 30 seconds or until smooth. Cover bowl and chill dough overnight.
3. About 2 hours before baking focacvia, line an 8 inch square baking pan or dish with parchment paper, bringing the paper up the sides. Dust parchment with 2 tbls flour. Pour chilled dough into pan. Cover with a clean kitchen towel and let rise in a warm place 11/2 to 2 hours or until doubled in volume.
4. Preheat oven to 425 F. Using damp fingers, press dimples all over the top of the focaccia . Add seasonings if you wish. I sprinkled some Italian seasoning, and Herbes de Provence.  
5. Bake 35 - 40 minutes or until browned and firm on top. Use the parchment paper to remove focaccia from pan; cool on a wire rack. Once cool enough handle, peel away parchment paper. Cool completely before serving.
8/1/25, 2:11 PM - +1 (408) 420-6174: does this turn out moist?
8/1/25, 2:22 PM - Gurmeet: Pea pasta with veggies.
Ingredients 
1. 3/4th packet of spaghetti ( cooked as per instructions on the box)
2. Garlic cloves- 4 chopped 
3. Red Onions- 1/2 chopped
4. Yellow squash- 1/4 chopped
5. Mushrooms- baby portabella 5 chopped
6. Tomatoes- 2 chopped 
7. Carrots- 2 chopped, medium sized
8. 21 salute seasoning - 1 tbls
9. Italian seasoning - 1 tbls
10. Dried parsley- 2 tsp
11. Salt free tomato paste- 3- 4 tbls
Method
1. To a pan, dry sauté garlic, onions. Add some water if needed. Cook until the onions turns translucent.  
2. Add the rest of the veggies,  cook with lid on until the veggies soften a little bit.
3. Add the chopped tomatoes, and the tomato paste, stir and cook with lid on until tomatoes soften. 
4. Take the lid off , add all the herbs,  and cook this sauce until it comes together and water dries up a little bit.
5. Stir in the cooked pasta, add some pasta water if it looks too dry. 
6. Pasta is ready! Enjoy!!

Notes- we may add bell pepper, zuchini, edmame to the pasta as well.
Add herbs of your choice.

1 cup ragi flour
5-6 large size ripe bananas
1 tsp baking powder
½ tsp baking soda
1/4 tsp salt
¼ cup walnuts/pecans/almonds chopped or crushed
1/4 cup plant yogurt
1/4 cup lukewarm water – can be replaced with vegan milk 
Extra walnuts/pecans/almonds or sugar free chocolate for garnish

8/6/25, 7:25 PM - Prathima WFPB: Pickle recipe: my mother in law's recipe. 

Vegetables of choice:
Lemon
Carrot
Bitter gourd
Jalapeño or chilli

Masala ingredients: (increase quantity depending on the amount of vegetables you choose)

1/2 tsp turmeric powder
Pinch of Hing 
2 tsp Chili powder
1 tsp Pepper powder
1/2 tsp Clove powder
2-3 tsp Raw Mustard seeds powder or yellow mustard seeds

Dry roast some garlic and mustard seeds. Mix it with the above ingredients with salt as needed.

Make a small amount if you are not using salt.

8/6/25, 7:28 PM - Prathima WFPB: Flaxseed chutney pudi:
1 cup roasted flaxseed, few garlic cloves roasted,  chili powder as per your taste, salt, raw coriander leaves few sticks, roasted curry leaves 1-2 stick, tamarind for sourness. Blend everything into powder.

Paprika can be used for kids.

8/7/25, 9:36 AM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: i use dark choclate bars 100 percent 
 
melt bar in double boiler
add a big spoon of cashew butter
then a big spoon of date paste 
i have this silicon choclate bar mould 

then i let them settle in freezer
kee it refrigerated all the time
once out at room temperature they start to loose the shape 
also they do not have that original choclate bar crunch 
they are soft only

8/7/25, 9:44 AM - Prathima WFPB: Zunka/Junka Vada:

Using mortar and pestle, crush 1/2 tsp cumin seeds, 2-3 green chillies, 1/2 inch ginger, 2-3 garlic pods, pinch of salt.

Heat a tawa  Dry roast mustard seeds, finely chopped onions and curry leaves, above crushed green chili etc, add 1/4th tsp turmeric powder, hing. Add 1/2 cup water to this and let it boil.

In a bowl, mix 1 cup besan with 1 cup water using a hand mixer or whisker. Add this besan mix to the boiling water and mix it till it cooks well for 5mins.

Once done, take a plate and add roasted sesame and poppy seeds, coriander leaves. Put the prepared zunka on it and using water tap it all around. Add toppings of sesame, poppy, coriander leaves again and cut it into desired shapes.

8/9/25, 10:04 AM - +1 (979) 739-9350: <Media omitted>
8/9/25, 10:06 AM - Kusum WFPB: This is a very good looking delicious bowl
8/9/25, 10:14 AM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: take wheat flour chana flour bajra flour in 3:2:2 ratio
you can also change the ratio 
just keep wheat flour a little more than other flours

than add salt, ajwain seeds and half tsp baking soda and one fourth tsp baking powder

mix

add  tbsp peanut butter (or any other nut butter, replacement of oil)

than make a nice dough with warm water

let sit for 10 min

use the dough to make desired shape or simply make a roti out of it and cut into squares with knife (depending on how much time you have 🫣)

now bake at 180 for 15 min
open the oven and shuffle them all and bake again for 10 min

let it cool and they'll turn Crispy

you could also add chilli powder, meethi or curry leaves
8/9/25, 10:16 AM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: also use a fork to make some holes before baking
8/9/25, 10:17 AM - Leena Menon WFPB: You are so creative. Your sweets always look so so attractive and yummy.
8/9/25, 10:21 AM - Deepti Khanna: Thank You !!
8/9/25, 10:21 AM - Shruti Mirashi Joshi: Looks great! How did you make it?
8/9/25, 10:23 AM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: 2 cups roasted fox nuts
half cup roasted chana

blend into fine powder

in a pan(preferably nonstick)
take half cup plant milk(i used cashew milk)
let it warm and then slowly add powder while stirring continously 

it will take good 10- 12 min to cook on medium flame
can add elachi powder
it will start to leave the pan then add half cup date paste and mix quickly
cook for another 2 min

and take it out on a plate

now you can eat it like that only  and turn into desired shape 

I divided the dough into 2 parts and added cocoa to one part and then gave this peda shape


*not my original recipe 
saw it on facebook and turned it into wfpb

the taste is exactly like mawa peda (if you still remember that flavour)

8/10/25, 10:50 AM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: *Here are the recipes*

*Dal:*
1 cup dal, washed
1 cup water
1/2 tsp turmeric 
1/4 tsp salt

Steam in instapot for 15 minutes. Add lemon juice before serving. 

*Sweet/sour zucchini:*
Take a thicker zucchini. (They hold their texture better. Can be made with pumpkin, or other hard squash) Cut into cubes. 3-4 cups
Spices: dry roast 1 tsp of cumin, mustard, fennel, kalonji seeds. Add coriander powder, chilli powder, turmeric and hing. Add the cut zucchini + chopped green chillies and ginger. 
Stir well, cover and simmer on medium low for 10-15 min. Once soft, add lemon juice, 1 tsp date syrup/paste, and cilantro. Stir in and serve. 

*Tofu*
Open a pack of tofu, drain it in the refrigerator overnight. (I open it, pour out all the liquid and stand it on its side in the container, then put it back in the fridge). After 7-8 hours, cut it into 6 slices and air fry at high for 8-10 minutes, or until lightly golden. Rub into sriracha mixed with date paste and air fry for another 8 minutes on each side at 400 degrees. 

*Yucca*
Peel and steam yucca for 15 minutes in Instapot. Cube into bite size pieces. Dry roast cumin in a pan. Fry some onion in it (add a splash of water). Add coriander powder, chilli, hing, turmeric etc with green chili and grated ginger. Add 1/2 cup soy yogurt and mix it in. Add yucca, mix thoroughly and cook for 10 minutes until tender and flavors are blended. Add in chopped cilantro and serve.  

Yucca tastes great just steamed too.

Like I have shared before, I use Instapot with smaller containers for steaming. If cooking directly in the pot, cooking time should be reduced. <This message was edited>

8/11/25, 4:33 PM - Prathima WFPB: Almond cookies:

2 cups almond flour
1/2 cup whole wheat flour
4-5 tbsp of silken tofu
1 cup dates powder
1tsp baking powder
Salt, optional
Herbs or 100% dark chocolate or chopped nuts, optional

Mix and bake below 180°C for 15-20 mins in a preheated oven. Turn the cookies in between.

8/12/25, 6:58 PM - Prathima WFPB: Masala powder:
Dry roast 1tbsp urad dal, 1tsp coriander seeds, red chilies 3-4, sesame seeds 1tbsp, 1 tbsp, curry few, cumin 1tsp, fennel seeds 1tsp, methi seeds 1/4th tsp, pepper 1tsp. Powder it into coarse or fine powder. Keep it aside. 

Dry roast mustard seeds, cumin seeds, curry leaves, add 1/2 cup soaked black or white chickpeas with 1 cup of water and cook it till it is half done, add chopped ivy gourd to it and cook it with a closed lid. Once cooked, add some cashews, above masala powder as per taste, turmeric powder and salt. Garnish with coriander leaves.

8/14/25, 11:03 AM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: Quite easy, really. Grate and steam about 1 cup lauki (I do it in the microwave for 3-4 min. This helps get rid of the moisture too). Cool it completely. Add it to slightly warm TJs soymilk (1 min in microwave) with the yogurt culture from a previous batch. Cover and leave undisturbed in a warm place for 4-5 hours or until set. (I use yogurt setting in Instapot for 4-5 hours. Then leave it on the counter for an additional 3-4 hours to get the tart flavour). <This message was edited>

8/15/25, 11:20 AM - +1 (832) 755-3722: Recipe-
Matar ka Nimona WFPBNO 

16-20 oz Petite peas frozen
3-4 medium yellow potatoes cubed
1 cup arugula (chopped fine)
2 tomatoes 
1 red onion 
2-4 cloves garlic 
1 inch ginger 
1-2 dates (optional)
Cumin seeds 1/4 tsp
Turmeric powder, Garam masala, cayenne pepper, paprika according to taste 
Cilantro for garnishing 
Kasuri methi (optional) 

Run the thawed frozen petite peas through a food processor to get a coarse texture (not a smooth paste).
In a pan, add cumin seeds, then add chopped onion, some salt saute for 2-3 minutes, add ginger garlic and sauce until onion is translucent. Add tomatoes, and saute until soft. 
Add 1-2 dates (optional) 
Cool a little and make a paste (not very smooth)
In a wok/pan, add a few cumin seeds, and then add the ground peas and cubed potatoes. Add some water and cover. Stir often. Add arugula. Cook for another 3-4 minutes until the potatoes are tender. 
Add salt, the tomato onion paste, and spices.  Cook for another 3-4 minutes. Add Kasuri methi if using. Turn off the gas. Garnish with cilantro. Enjoy!

8/16/25, 8:55 PM - Prathima WFPB: Vegan Orange Chocolate Cake

A wholesome cake with Vitamin C rich oranges, dates and whole grains. It can be offered to toddlers and is even diabetic friendly. Dates are best sweetener as it has natural fibre as well. 
Recipe:

Dry ingredients:
Brown/ Red rice flour 3/4th cup (If not available then blend oats into flour or Jowar or Ragi flour) 
Gram flour or Besan 1 cup
Cacao or cocoa powder 1/4th cup
Flaxseed powder 1-2 tsp
Baking soda 1 tsp

Wet ingredients:
Orange juice with pulp 1 1/2 cup
Dates paste 1 1/2 cup or as per sweetness
Apple cider vinegar or lemon juice 2 tsp
Orange/lemon zest 1 tsp as per flavour

Frosting
Dates paste 4 tsp
Cacao or Cocoa powder 1/4th cup
Oats 1/4th cup (or Cashew or Almonds) 
Orange zest 1/2 tsp
Water 
Method:
1. Sieve brown rice flour, besan, cocoa powder. Add flaxseed powder and soda and mix well. 
2. Blend 3-4 oranges to get around 1 1/2 cup juice, do not strain as fibre is important. 
Blend dates into smooth paste with minimal water. Add orange juice, lemon juice, zest and blend again. 
3. Mix dry and wet ingredients so that it gets combined well. Do not over mix as it'll make cake denser. 
4. Pour into cake moulds and bake below 160°C for 30-40 mins or put a skewer to check if comes out clean. 
Without oven:
Take a idli vessel, fill base with sand or soda. Pour batter into mould or a vessel and bake till it's cooked well. ( I did this way as I do not have access to oven) 
Frosting:
Blend oats into flour. Add 1 cup water and blend it into milk like consistency. Add cocoa powder, dates, zest and blend again. Add more dates paste as per sweetness. Warm this to thicken the sauce. Pour it on cake and serve.

Other frosting options: cashew, cocoa powder, dates, soy milk.

Purple doll dress frosting: cashew, frozen blueberry, sweetener of choice.
8/16/25, 10:20 PM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: I am probably going to improvise the recipe but here’s what I did:

pumpkin, sunflower, apricot, other melon seeds(other seeds optional)
chia and hemp seeds
chopped dried cranberries
chopped raisins
roasted gond/edible gum
powdered date sugar
date syrup
water 

Mix everything together except water. Best to do by hand. Add water only if it is needed to bind the mix so it becomes a complete mass. Press it down on parchment paper in a baking tray, about 1/4” thick. Bake at 275 for 25-30 minutes. Cool completely before serving. <This message was edited>

8/17/25, 7:35 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: *Coconut Substitutes*: Coconuts are a cherished food for many of us! But coconuts are discouraged by WFPB luminaries like Ornish, Barnard, Esselstyn, McDougall, Peter Rogers, …! What to do? Here are some ideas by members of our recipe sharing group:

* _"I am a Mangalorean so traditionally we use a lot of coconut in our cooking...1 way of reducing the quantity of coconut consumption in the cooking is by grinding Silken tofu with maybe 1 tbsp or 2 of coconut blend it and u will get the desired coconut flavor and consistency that u need for ur south indian gravy dishes"_ — Sharmila Vedam  (@⁨~Sharmila⁩) [PCRM Food For Life Coach]

* _"I grind a tiny amount of coconut (for flavor) with roasted daliya [taste and texture is same as coconut based gravy.         But no matter which essence I buy, the only flavor I get is that of KoprKopra matter which essence I buy, the only flavor I get is that of Koprayonut)."_ — Padma (@⁨Padma WFPB⁩)

* _"A surprising substitute I stumbled upon recently. Fig leaves have a hint of coconut in their flavor. More young coconut flavor than mature coconut. I used it to wrap food I was steaming as I’d run out but I have a small fig tree in the garden. I used it in a dessert."_ — Uma (@⁨Uma Venkataraman WFPB⁩)

* _"Celery (plus hemp or cashews) is a great substitute for coconut. I use itAmbikahe time in koottu, chutneys, and pachadis (various sauce like dishes in s.Indian cuisine). WilAmbikaout sambar and share if it turns out good. For sambar, coconut is not necessary if fresh masala is made, so have not tried celery there as yet."_ — Ambika (@⁨~Ambika⁩)

* _"For south indian ‘morekuzhambu’ soaked raw peanuts also work.     _ — AmbikaowlAmbikaceeeds while blending which usually masks the flavor of coconut substitutes i use. I would slowlAmbikace the coconut and substitute with alternate stuff until you strike the right taste for you."_ — Ambika (@⁨~Ambika⁩)

Two posts in this Whole Food Plant-Based support group on Facebook discuss coconut substitutes:

* _"Most of the recipes from Mangalore contain coconut. I have reduced the amount of coconut but I still feel I need it in traditional foods. Has anyone found a good substitute for coconut in gravies?"_ (Post 1 in Plant Based Desi (Facebook)) https://www.facebook.com/groups/PLANTBASEDDESI/permalink/1925327741155829

* _"Made mallu “stew” for idlis tonite. A hack for coconut milk - used almond milk and added few drops of coconut extract."_ (Post 2 in Plant Based Desi (Facebook)) https://www.facebook.com/groups/PLANTBASEDDESI/permalink/1277226865965923

8/19/25, 1:59 PM - +1 (415) 706-2250: SOLIGT Stackable Stainless Steel Seed Sprouting Kit, 2-Tier Mesh Sprouting Trays, 1 Base and 1 Cover Lid 
 https://a.co/d/9ElGJmv

I recently got these sprouting trays because I am trying to get away from my plastic trays. I also have jars but prefer trays for larger sprouts such as broccoli, lentil and beans. These metal trays have a wire mesh as a base which allows for fast drainage. A disadvantage is that the sprouts will grow through the mesh and can get stuck in there. I deal with that by twice daily dumping the whole tray content into a bowl with some filtered water and gently removing any stuck sprouts. I clean out any hulls and the sprouts come out beautifully.

8/20/25, 9:54 AM - Prathima WFPB: This one was with eyeball measurements as I made it with my daughter.

Base: almond flour, cashew, walnuts, vanilla powder, dates. Coarsely powder these and tap it in the mould and bake them for a few minutes or use it raw itself and create the base layer. (Note: corsely blend nuts before adding dates in the end)

Mango layer: frozen and fresh mango, cashews. One layer of only mango puree, topped with chopped mango. Dates were not needed as mango was ripe. U can add dates if extra sweetness is needed.

Blueberry layer: frozen blueberry, any nuts, dates. Topped with frozen Blueberry.

My daughter loved it.

8/21/25, 7:04 AM - Gurmeet: I usually soak it overnight, then boil it either in a pot, or a cooker. It takes 25-30 minutes in a pot and 10 minutes after whistle in a cooker. I add dates+water( blended) for sweetner+cardamom powder+all spice powder+cinnamon to this. Top it off with fruits and enjoy! I made this yesterday for the kids and they loved it. Adding plant based milk is optional. 
When cooking in a pot, stir it constantly,  it becomes thick. I liked the taste of the one cooked in a pot better than the cooker version. Hope this helps.

8/21/25, 11:46 AM - Gurmeet: Tofu Bhurjee- firm tofu, water drained and crumbled, spring onions, red onion chopped, garlic, zuchini chopped, baby spinach,  goda masala, coriander+cumin powder, turmeric powder, red chili powder, green chili, mint leaves, cilantro, tomato chopped, snap peas, red radish.

Dry sauté the chopped onions+the spring onion bulb, add the rest of the vegetables,  and spinach.  Cook with lid on for 3-4 minutes.  Now add the tofu, tomatoes, mint leaves, and all the dry spice powders. Stir and cook until water dries up a little bit. Garnish with Cilantro.

***we may add any vegetables of our choice..mushrooms, bell peppers, broccoli. <This message was edited>

8/25/25, 8:04 AM - Shruti Mirashi Joshi: I just realized that the last step for this is missing. 
You should be pouring sweetened plant based milk while serving the cake or even soak the cake in sweetened milk. That's what makes it like Tres Leches and will make it sweeter.

I will give you a few options to make sweetened soy milk:

1. Add thick soaked cashew and raisins paste to the soy milk
2.  Add maple syrup and cashew butter to the soy milk 

3. Add date syrup and cashew butter to the soy milk

I had not added cashews in the milk but I recommend it for an enhanced dessert eating experience.

Just soy milk and the sweetener of your choice should work. <This message was edited>

8/28/25, 1:28 PM - Kusum WFPB: I tried a tablespoon of seeds 
Soaked in water for 24 hours, change water by draining and adding fresh water in 12 hours 
Then drained water via strainer and and let it sit in the strainer overnight and cover it with a lid 
Rinse in the strainer and then leave it again for another 10-12 hours 
Then leave the seeds on a paper towel and cover it lightly to allow air circulation 
I think if you have the sprouting jar then it should work as well. I don’t have the sprouting jar.
This is my first time making this sprout as an experimenting process <This message was edited>

8/29/25, 10:21 AM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: Yes @⁨Gurmeet⁩ 😛 here you go(just finished an 8 day water fast). 

*My falafel recipe*
2 cups dried chickpeas (Do NOT use canned or cooked chickpeas)
1 cup fresh parsley leaves, stems removed
3/4 cup fresh cilantro
1/2 cup fresh dill, stems removed
1 medium white onion, quartered
2-3 garlic cloves, peeled
1 tsp ground black pepper
1 tbsp ground cumin
1 tbsp ground coriander
1 tsp cayenne pepper
1 tsp baking powder
1/2 tsp baking soda
2 tbsp toasted sesame seeds
2-3 tbsp rice flour
Salt to taste

Soak the chickpeas for 7-8 hours or overnight. Run everything except sesame seeds, rice flour and salt in a food processor until it resembles medium breadcrumbs. Take out in a bowl and add the sesame seeds, rice flour and salt. Mix well. Form into flat 1.5 inch round discs. Air fry at nugget setting at 350° for 10 minutes each side.  Serve with fresh babaghanouj, hummus and salad. <This message was edited>

9/2/25, 12:01 PM - +1 (551) 208-2647: Can you please share the cooking process for jowar, including soaking time and cooking methods using an Instant Pot or pressure cooker? When I cooked jowar, the outer layer split, and I prefer it to remain intact and soft.
9/2/25, 12:30 PM - Satvic Swetha Kogatam: Sure ! 
1. Wash Jowar (1.5cup) and soak overnight .
2. Throw away the soaked water , added a handful of peanuts and I pressure cooked with 8-9 whistles with 3-4 cups of water ( insta pot , the bean chili mode might work ! ) ( check if the Jowar can be mashed with two fingers to see if it’s cooked , else couple more whistles)
3. ⁠In a pan , dry sauté the mustard , Jeera , urad dal , Greenchilli , onion(I skipped it) , red chili , curry leaf .
4. ⁠Added veggies  - carrot , peas , corn etc and add salt and cook it for 2-3 mins . 
5. ⁠Add boiled Jowar discarding the water and cook it for another 3-4 mins on medium flame with the lid on . 
6. ⁠I sprinkled some sesame , some roasted peanuts, chopped coriander , lemon juice and Done ! ( Can add grated fresh coconut for satvic version , skip for wfpb version ) <This message was edited>

9/2/25, 8:04 PM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: *Apple Plum & Beetroot Jam* by mom ✨😍
* ⁠Boil or steam 3 cups of small pieces of apple, beetroot and whole plums (deseeded).
* Grind or blend them with 1 cup deseeded soaked dates. 
* Cook the mixture with 2 cloves, 1/4 tsp ginger powder, 1/4 tsp cinnamon powder and 1 bay leaf, stirring continuously till it becomes thick consistency.
* Turn off the flame.
* Add 1/2 tsp cardamom powder. Mix well.
* Cool it and serve it with Whole Food Plant-Based bread, roti, paratha, puri or cookies!

9/6/25, 12:48 PM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: I got a super ripe pineapple 
so thought of trying it

very simple and it turned out really well

200 gms rolled oats
100 gms of pineapple (recipe says pineapple juice but i used chunks only)
50 gms peanut butter
pinch of salt
1tsp baking powder
100-150 ml water (approx)
100 gms dates
pulse everything together

thin pineapple slices at the bottom of pan
batter
second layer of pineapple slice
rest of the batter

baked at 180 for 50 min

after 50 min I flipped it over and baked for another 10 min 

now i felt it to be little sweet for my family's pallet

so I made this cream
 
which is a boiled potato, handful of cashews, 2 dates, some water
vanilla extract
blend nicely <This message was edited>

9/10/25, 11:45 AM - +1 (774) 701-3068: We used to make using blender and then muslin cloth. 
But past 2 years, we got a NAMA juicer that makes the process much easier and less messy.

Since soy milk needs boiling right after making it, it is also best to take a part of it and set yogurt right then.

We soak organic soybeans (from amazon), take a quick boil of the soaked beans before milking them. Reduces the strong flavor.

9/30/25, 9:44 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: We may search for “Nakiri Knife” — several brands have it. I would suggest splurging some money on this purchase (upwards of $100). Buy a honing rod as well to hone the knife periodically (and occasionally sharpen it).

I have only two knives with me: (a) Nakiri knife, and (b) Utility knife. The utility knife can be simple — no need buy a high quality one. The Nakiri knife is my main knife that I use for {slicing, chopping, dicing, mincing} several pounds of fruits & vegetables daily.

Also buy a large sized wooden cutting board. High quality ones are made of teak. <This message was edited>

10/1/25, 10:12 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: Shall we make a recipe card for air fried banana chips?
10/1/25, 10:12 AM - Gurmeet Singh Manku: <Media omitted>
10/1/25, 10:23 AM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: yes please... 🙂

infact I tried many things to come up with this recipe 
so
thinly slice raw bananas (these are 500gms)
put them in a box

in a bowl take 2-3tbsp of jowar aata
salt to taste 
some spices like black pepper, garlic powder, red chilli powder (depends on  the flavours you want)

now sprinkle some water on banana chips
add the spice mix
close the lid of box and shake the box so that all the chips are nicely coated with spice mix

 store  the box with lid closed in the freezer for 2 hours

air fryer them in a pre heated air fryer at 200 degree for 15-18 min with giving it a shake 2 times in between
10/1/25, 10:25 AM - +1 (416) 998-0434: Can we please get the recipe for the cookies as well 😃
10/1/25, 10:49 AM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: Breakfast Cookies 8-10 cookies

Ingredients

Dry
oats flour– 1 cup (100 g)
ragi flour – ½ cup (60 g)
Chana flour – ½ cup (60 g)
protein powder (soy/pea) – 1 cup (70 g)
Cinnamon – 1 tsp
Salt – ½ tsp
Baking powder – ½ tsp
Baking soda 1/2 tsp

Wet
Date paste ½ cup (120 g)
Flaxseed meal – 3 tbsp + 9 tbsp water (flax eggs)
Plant milk – 6 tbsp (90 ml, adjust)
Vanilla extract – 1 tsp


Nuts & Seeds
Almonds – 2 tbsp (chopped)
Cashews – 2 tbsp (chopped)
Pumpkin seeds – 2 tbsp
Sunflower seeds – 2 tbsp
Chia seeds – 1 tbsp


Method

1. Make flax eggs (3 tbsp flaxseed meal + 9 tbsp water, rest 5 min).
2. Mix dry ingredients.
3. Stir wet ingredients together.
4. Combine wet + dry → thick dough (adjust with milk/flour).
5. Fold in nuts & seeds.
6. Shape 8-10 cookies 
7. Bake 180 °C (350 °F) for 15–18 min.
8. Cool before storing.

for choclate flavour
add some carob powder or cocoa  to the cookie dough

for lemon cookies 
add some lemon zest and few drops of lemon juice to the dough

10/15/25, 5:43 PM - +91 98885 10132: Lovely colourful presentation 👌👌❤️❤️
10/15/25, 6:01 PM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: Sure
10/15/25, 6:01 PM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: for cake

dry ingredients 
100 gm oat flour 
20-30 gms Ragi flour 
pinch of salt
1 tsp baking powder 
half tsp baking soda 
20-25 gms cocoa


wet ingredients 
100 gms sweet potato boiled 
100 gms dates without seeds
50-100 ml water (as required for smooth consistency)
Half tsp vinegar

blend dry and wet ingredients separately and then mix by cut and fold method 
bake for 40 min at 180
10/15/25, 6:02 PM - Gunjan Sahni WFPB: for frosting

100 gms Boiled sweet potato 
50 gms dates
15-20 gms cocoa
50 gms nut butter
pinch of salt
about 100 ml of non dairy milk for proper blending of ingredients 

blend and cool

let the cake cool completely before putting frosting 

Again let the ready cake with frosting sit in the fridge for 2-3 hours for perfect taste

can decorate with 
Dark chocolate and nuts

10/29/25, 11:49 AM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: I don’t always follow a recipe 😛 it depends on what I have in the fridge/freezer and what I can come up with. I had eaten this soup years ago in northern Italy and have ever since tried to replicate the taste 😋. 

You can totally use other herbs like oregano, parsley, sage, thyme, any white beans and veggies like carrots, celery, mushrooms, other greens like chard, kale. (Broccoli and cauliflower etc will get too mushy or add them towards the end of cooking. I like a little bite to the veges). There’s no tomato in this soup. 

I sautéed one large red onion for a few minutes.  Then added about 2 cups watermelon rinds, 
1 cup collard stems, 
2 cloves of garlic

Let it cook to soften. Then added the bell peppers and 2 cups soaked and boiled white lima beans (2 cups cooked) and black pepper with some salt. Once all cooked nicely, I added chopped spinach and basil leaves and stirred them in. Topped with red pepper flakes. Need to eat it right when it’s done for the best taste. 😋

Notice there’s no pasta in it 😝. Instead I added barley from making barley tea because I needed to finish it. <This message was edited>

10/29/25, 3:23 PM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: 🤣 roast barley grains until they turn medium brown. Store in an airtight container once cooled. Take 2 cups water and 2tbsp barley in a pot. Bring to a boil and steep at low for 5 minutes. Strain it and enjoy! You can add the barley grains to salads, soups or just eat them.

10/29/25, 4:43 PM - Ankit Dangi WFPB: In the early days, I likely phased in new ingredients gradually, typically adding one or two newer ingredient at a time. 

On indigestion: not to the variety of ingredients. However, it varies with each ingredient. For instance, if one is not used to eating several fruits in one meal, eating red grapes and prunes can be gassy in the early days; if one is not used to having spicy peppers such as habaneros or ghost peppers, it can cause burning sensations during bowel movements. 

I suppose, general rules of thumb apply here too: when starting sth new, start small. :) <This message was edited>

10/31/25, 10:45 AM - +1 (847) 830-4555: Can you share the method that you utilized?
10/31/25, 10:47 AM - +1 (979) 739-9350: Please share recipe. 🙏
10/31/25, 1:19 PM - Prathima WFPB: Firm tofu frozen for 1-2 days.
Defrost it in fridge for 1 day.

Use a cotton towel or napkin to press any extra water or keep weights.
Once done, chop tofu into desired shapes. 

Make a marinade with 1tsp corn flour, 2-3 tsp water, 1tsp soy sauce, pepper powder, 1tsp vinegar.
Add tofu to this and let it sit for 15-30mins.

In airfryer, bake the marinated tofu for 8-10 mins at 350°F or until it is crispy on the outside. Keep it aside. Use it in any recipes.

Stir fry:
Using a food chopper, chop lots of onion, garlic, ginger, green chili.

On a well heated pan, roast the above veggies and bell pepper or anything else u like, add remaining marinade to this and added baked tofu. add 1tsp soy sauce if needed or any seasoning of choice. 1/2 tsp dates paste or maple syrup can be added for lighter sweet taste <This message was edited>

11/10/25, 6:32 PM - Shruti Mirashi Joshi: Looks great! How did you make spices stick to the mix?
11/10/25, 6:38 PM - Gurmeet: Chatgpt came to the rescue.  It is sort of sticking. I will try plant milk next time. It tastes great though. The makhanna and mamra are infused with the flavor of the spices. 

1. Dry roast the base:

Roast mamra and makhana separately in a wide pan on low heat until crisp. Remove and set aside.



2. Prepare the spice coating:

In a small bowl, mix all the dry spices together.

Add 1–2 tbsp of warm water or plant milk to make a slightly thick paste — not watery.



3. Coat evenly:

Add the roasted mamra/makhana into a large mixing bowl.

Sprinkle the spice paste on top and toss quickly and gently using a spatula or your hands until everything is evenly coated.

If it feels too dry and the spices aren’t sticking, spray or sprinkle a little more water and toss again.



4. Re-roast to crisp up:

Spread the mixture on a baking sheet or pan.

Roast for 5–8 minutes in a preheated oven (300°F / 150°C) or on a low stove flame, stirring occasionally, until completely dry and crisp.

11/15/25, 8:57 PM - Ankit Dangi WFPB: What spices or spice mixes go into each of these preparations so that it tastes Ethiopian as compared to Indian? 🤔 <This message was edited>
11/16/25, 5:39 AM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: My MIL makes this and I loved it the first time I had it so make it whenever I can find large amounts of fresh methi. To make it WFPB, roast some mustard and sesame seeds (I put generous amounts of sesame), add hing, coriander powder, turmeric and Kashmiri chilli (add a few spoons of water right away so the powdered spices don’t get burnt). Then add cleaned and chopped methi. Stir, cover and let it cook in its own steam on medium low for 25-30 minutes or until soft. You can add a couple tbsps of roasted besan at the end of cooking. 

I love to stuff this in all kind of rotis. 

Methi tastes great cooked with split urad dal or split mung dal. 

You can make methi fritters with chopped onions, finely diced potatoes and besan and spices - air fried. 

People have mentioned methi parathas - you add cleaned and chopped methi (generous amounts - a whole bunch or more) to the flour before kneading the dough. 

Methi aloo is a popular dish in many parts of India. You can find recipes online. It tasted best when potatoes are cooked with the methi and not preboiled. 

The methi stems are tough, fibrous and hard to cook so it’s important to take off the leaves (like we do with mint). Don’t be tempted to keep the stems.
11/16/25, 5:58 AM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: @⁨Ankit Dangi WFPB⁩ - a great question.  Sorry @⁨Gurmeet Singh Manku⁩ for the long post but I think it will help people who want to make Ethiopian recipes. 

It is not that different from Indian and still when I make it, it does not taste like Indian. 

The yellow lentils (not chana dal) that you see in the picture are not used in Indian cooking. They are called yellow split peas and cook to a creamy texture and have a distinct taste. 

The main difference is the spice mix called berbere. The spices used are almost the same in this mix that we use in Indian cooking. However it is the proportions that account for the difference in taste. I have been making it for so long that I am able to intuitively add all the spices (like most things I make). I don’t use premade spice mixes (in most of my cooking). I’ll share the recipe for berbere below. 

Also these dishes are simply cooked. I steam the vegetables in the microwave for 10-12 minutes before sautéing. There is no premade ginger-garlic paste(I hate that thing with a passion - not because of taste but for the way it is made and used, and would get it banned if I could 🫨) and no tomatoes. This is how simple Indian food used to be, and still is in my mom’s kitchen, that I grew up on - without garlic and tomatoes. 

The combination of vegetables that I make is usually what we find at Ethiopian restaurants. I am sure Ethiopians eat many other vegetables (just like the standard Indian restaurant menu is a far cry from what Indians eat everyday). 

I hope this helps. <This message was edited>

11/26/25, 11:32 AM - Gurmeet: No-Coconut WFPB Olan

Ingredients:

1 cup ash gourd (white pumpkin), diced

½ cup red cowpeas (vanpayar / red lobia), soaked overnight and cooked

1 small green chili, slit

A few curry leaves

Salt to taste (optional or minimal)


For the creamy base (coconut substitute):

¼ cup cooked brown rice or rolled oats

2 tbsp unsalted raw cashews or 2 tbsp cooked white beans (for nut-free)

¾–1 cup warm water



---

Instructions:

1. Cook cowpeas until soft and tender.


2. Cook ash gourd separately with green chili, curry leaves, and a splash of water until tender.


3. Blend the creamy base:

Combine cooked rice (or oats), cashews (or white beans), and water.

Blend until smooth and milky — this mimics the richness of coconut milk.



4. Add the creamy blend to the cooked vegetables and beans.


5. Simmer gently for 3–4 minutes to let flavors meld (do not boil hard).


6. Finish with a few fresh curry leaves for aroma.

11/27/25, 7:10 AM - Shalini Other Lady WFPB: An interesting lentil soup/stew from *Jose Andres*. I deleted the part where he adds 3/4 cup of olive oil to saute the onions 😱

*My Lentil Stew*
Serves 6-8 (or 4 plus leftovers)

1 16-ounce bag brown lentils, rinsed and picked over
1 large tomato
1 medium red onion, peeled
1 leek, trimmed and washed
3 celery ribs, trimmed
1 small head of garlic
5 medium carrots, peeled and trimmed, divided

1 large white onion, finely chopped
2 tablespoons pimentón (Spanish smoked paprika)…or more if you like
4 small potatoes, peeled and cut into 1/2-inch dice
Kosher salt
Sherry vinegar (optional)

Put the rinsed lentils in a large pot and cover with 4 cups of water. Add the tomato, red onion, leek, celery, garlic and 2 of the carrots. Add 1/4 cup of the olive oil and more water if necessary, making sure all the vegetables are covered. Bring lentils and vegetables to a boil over medium-high heat, then reduce heat and simmer for 20 minutes. 

Occasionally skim the foam from the top of the boiling lentils and discard.

Saute the chopped onions and cook until golden and the onions just begin to caramelize, about 4 minutes. Add the pimentón, or whatever spices you have available, and stir until combined with the onions. Cook for about 30 seconds more then add the onions to the pot with the lentils and stir together until well combined. Continue to simmer the lentils for another 20 minutes.
Using tongs or a slotted spoon, carefully remove the celery, leek, tomato, garlic, & whole onion from the soup, and place them in a blender with some of the broth from the lentils. Puree the ingredients until smooth, then pour the mixture back into the pot and stir together with the lentils to thicken the soup. Cut the remaining 3 carrots into 1/2-inch thick slices and add them, along with the potatoes, to the soup and continue cooking until the vegetables are soft and the lentils are tender, about 10 more minutes. Season to taste with salt. Serve with a drizzle of sherry vinegar and a generous sprinkle of pimentón, if you like.

Happy thanksgiving!

12/1/25, 11:35 AM - +1 (408) 394-9053: Please share the process if it's not too much trouble. Did you
12/1/25, 11:53 AM - Ankit Dangi WFPB: If you’re asking for the hot chocolate, here’s what I did today: I added dried fruits (for sweetener; I had sun-dried apricots. You may use dates or other dried fruits you have in your pantry), flax seeds, hemp hearts, pistachios, and walnuts (for creaminess; you may skip, omit or reduce some of these depending on the taste and texture you like — I added flax seeds and walnuts cos, so far, I am not very diligent about their continued use in my meals on a regular basis) to a blender with soy milk, and blended them into a purée. In a sauce pan, I added unsweetened cocoa powder, and cinnamon powder. Turned on the flame. Kept a cup of water in the microwave. I let the powders roast on low flame, until the water warms up in the microwave. Added the warm water, kept whisking it on low flame, and I let it thicken. I then, added an alcohol-free vanilla flavor, whisked it, and added the blended purée, while the flame is still on low. It is likely to thicken pretty fast (I rinse the blender with some water, and add it to the saucepan to balance the thickness, and helps rinse the blender). After turning off the flame, I added a sprinkle of ground red chipotle powder to give it a hot, spicy kick (I recollect that in South America, not sure which country, they use red chilli peppers in hot chocolate with cinnamon — you might be able to find a non-WFPB video on YouTube). Please feel free to balance the proportions of ingredients to your liking. It’ll likely be a few misses until you find the right proportions and ingredients of what you really like. ✨ <This message was edited>
